My fishing stuff is still sitting in the garage, the woodshop feels more like work than something I want to do, and when my kids ask me to play chess, I’ll sit with them, but I’m kind of just moving the pieces. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I try not to sit around blaming myself, but it’s hard not to.\n\nI keep thinking I’ve let everybody down—my wife, the kids, the guys at work. Like if I miss an appointment or something goes over budget, or there’s some safety issue on a job, I take it really personally. Probably too personally. It feels like proof that I’m… not doing any of this right. Like maybe I shouldn’t be the one in charge of anything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, a little. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m not trying to scare anybody or anything, but… on those early drives, before anyone else is awake, I’ll catch myself thinking it might be easier if I just didn’t make it there.\n\nAnd sometimes the thought of steering off the road comes up, and that scares me because it feels… too close, I guess. I don’t like saying that out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year and a half?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit, I guess. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches.\n\nFood just doesn’t really do much for me lately. Most days it kind of tastes like cardboard, so I’ll skip breakfast, and then even after being out on a job site all day I’ll just pick at dinner. It’s probably not great, I know.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or restless you feel, or if you've been feeling slowed down compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean… I don’t think I’ve been pacing around or anything like that.\n\nSlowed down… maybe a little? But I don’t know if that’s just normal tiredness or me getting older, you know. I still get myself where I need to be. I probably don’t notice it as much as someone else would. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being flat or dulled out lasted for more than two years now?\n\nPatient: Not more than two years, no. It’s been… about a year and seven months, I think.\n\nIt really started after my wife’s health problems changed things at home. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before like anyone, but this flat, dulled-out feeling… that’s been since then, roughly. Is that the kind of timeframe you’re asking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling really down or depressed before the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any big way. I mean, I’ve had bad weeks here and there, like anybody, but I wouldn’t say I had real periods of being depressed before this.\n\nAt least, not that I’d want to call it that. Does that answer it the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worried or anxious about things?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I’m an anxious person, exactly. I do worry about things, sure, but I’ve always felt like that’s pretty normal.\n\nLately maybe I get a little caught up in my head sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxiety. I can’t really tell if it’s more than anyone else would have. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think there’s been, like, a clear separate period where it got dramatically worse than now.\n\nIt’s more been… kind of steady, I guess. Some days are better or worse, but I don’t know that I’d call it a whole period of being significantly worse. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Honestly… not much lately. I wish I could say there was something, but I kind of just don’t look forward to the things I used to.

Maybe about a year and a half—closer to a year and seven months, I think. It really started after my wife’s health problems changed everything at home. I kept thinking I’d adjust and it would pass, but… yeah, it’s been going on since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it as sad most of the time, exactly. Maybe kind of flat, or… I don’t know, dulled out? I don’t want to overstate it. Some days I can seem okay, I think. It’s not like I hate those things now or anything. I just… don’t feel much from them. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’ve just kind of stopped caring about things I used to look forward to. It probably sounds silly, but my fishing gear’s been sitting untouched in the garage, and even going out to the woodshop feels like a chore now. My kids try to get me to play chess with them, and I do it, but… I’m mostly just going through the motions. I figured maybe that’s worth talking about?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while, honestly. Maybe about a year and a half—closer to a year and seven months, I think. It really started after my wife’s health problems changed everything at home. I kept thinking I’d adjust and it would pass, but… yeah, it’s been going on since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it as sad most of the time, exactly. Maybe kind of flat, or… I don’t know, dulled out? I don’t want to overstate it. Some days I can seem okay, I think. It’s just been hard to feel much of anything about things, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable, or has the pleasure gone away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wish I could say I still did, but not really. It’s not like I hate those things now or anything. I just… don’t feel much from them. My fishing stuff is still sitting in the garage, the woodshop feels more like work than something I want to do, and when my kids ask me to play chess, I’ll sit with them, but I’m kind of just moving the pieces. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I try not to sit around blaming myself, but it’s hard not to.\n\nI keep thinking I’ve let everybody down—my wife, the kids, the guys at work. Like if I miss an appointment or something goes over budget, or there’s some safety issue on a job, I take it really personally. Probably too personally. It feels like proof that I’m… not doing any of this right. Like maybe I shouldn’t be the one in charge of anything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, a little. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m not trying to scare anybody or anything, but… on those early drives, before anyone else is awake, I’ll catch myself thinking it might be easier if I just didn’t make it there.\n\nAnd sometimes the thought of steering off the road comes up, and that scares me because it feels… too close, I guess. I don’t like saying that out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year and a half?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit, I guess. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches.\n\nFood just doesn’t really do much for me lately. Most days it kind of tastes like cardboard, so I’ll skip breakfast, and then even after being out on a job site all day I’ll just pick at dinner. It’s probably not great, I know.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or restless you feel, or if you've been feeling slowed down compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean… I don’t think I’ve been pacing around or anything like that.\n\nSlowed down… maybe a little? But I don’t know if that’s just normal tiredness or me getting older, you know. I still get myself where I need to be. I probably don’t notice it as much as someone else would. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being flat or dulled out lasted for more than two years now?\n\nPatient: Not more than two years, no. It’s been… about a year and seven months, I think.\n\nIt really started after my wife’s health problems changed things at home. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before like anyone, but this flat, dulled-out feeling… that’s been since then, roughly. Is that the kind of timeframe you’re asking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling really down or depressed before the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any big way. I mean, I’ve had bad weeks here and there, like anybody, but I wouldn’t say I had real periods of being depressed before this.\n\nAt least, not that I’d want to call it that. Does that answer it the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worried or anxious about things?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I’m an anxious person, exactly. I do worry about things, sure, but I’ve always felt like that’s pretty normal.\n\nLately maybe I get a little caught up in my head sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxiety. I can’t really tell if it’s more than anyone else would have. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think there’s been, like, a clear separate period where it got dramatically worse than now.\n\nIt’s more been… kind of steady, I guess. Some days are better or worse, but I don’t know that I’d call it a whole period of being significantly worse. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I try to still do things, especially if the kids ask, but it doesn’t feel the same.\n\nI used to really look forward to fishing, messing around in the woodshop, that kind of thing. Yeah… I think I have. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I just don’t really care about the things I used to look forward to.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF33.1, F33.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’ve just kind of stopped caring about things I used to look forward to. It probably sounds silly, but my fishing gear’s been sitting untouched in the garage, and even going out to the woodshop feels like a chore now. My kids try to get me to play chess with them, and I do it, but… I’m mostly just going through the motions. I figured maybe that’s worth talking about?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while, honestly. Maybe about a year and a half—closer to a year and seven months, I think. It really started after my wife’s health problems changed everything at home. I kept thinking I’d adjust and it would pass, but… yeah, it’s been going on since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it as sad most of the time, exactly. Maybe kind of flat, or… I don’t know, dulled out? I don’t want to overstate it. Some days I can seem okay, I think. It’s just been hard to feel much of anything about things, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable, or has the pleasure gone away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wish I could say I still did, but not really. It’s not like I hate those things now or anything. I just… don’t feel much from them. My fishing stuff is still sitting in the garage, the woodshop feels more like work than something I want to do, and when my kids ask me to play chess, I’ll sit with them, but I’m kind of just moving the pieces. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I try not to sit around blaming myself, but it’s hard not to.\n\nI keep thinking I’ve let everybody down—my wife, the kids, the guys at work. Like if I miss an appointment or something goes over budget, or there’s some safety issue on a job, I take it really personally. Probably too personally. It feels like proof that I’m… not doing any of this right. Like maybe I shouldn’t be the one in charge of anything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, a little. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m not trying to scare anybody or anything, but… on those early drives, before anyone else is awake, I’ll catch myself thinking it might be easier if I just didn’t make it there.\n\nAnd sometimes the thought of steering off the road comes up, and that scares me because it feels… too close, I guess. I don’t like saying that out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year and a half?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit, I guess. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches.\n\nFood just doesn’t really do much for me lately. Most days it kind of tastes like cardboard, so I’ll skip breakfast, and then even after being out on a job site all day I’ll just pick at dinner. It’s probably not great, I know.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or restless you feel, or if you've been feeling slowed down compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean… I don’t think I’ve been pacing around or anything like that.\n\nSlowed down… maybe a little? But I don’t know if that’s just normal tiredness or me getting older, you know. I still get myself where I need to be. I probably don’t notice it as much as someone else would. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being flat or dulled out lasted for more than two years now?\n\nPatient: Not more than two years, no. It’s been… about a year and seven months, I think.\n\nIt really started after my wife’s health problems changed things at home. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before like anyone, but this flat, dulled-out feeling… that’s been since then, roughly. Is that the kind of timeframe you’re asking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling really down or depressed before the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any big way. I mean, I’ve had bad weeks here and there, like anybody, but I wouldn’t say I had real periods of being depressed before this.\n\nAt least, not that I’d want to call it that. Does that answer it the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worried or anxious about things?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I’m an anxious person, exactly. I do worry about things, sure, but I’ve always felt like that’s pretty normal.\n\nLately maybe I get a little caught up in my head sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxiety. I can’t really tell if it’s more than anyone else would have. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think there’s been, like, a clear separate period where it got dramatically worse than now.\n\nIt’s more been… kind of steady, I guess. Some days are better or worse, but I don’t know that I’d call it a whole period of being significantly worse. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I try to still do things, especially if the kids ask, but it doesn’t feel the same.\n\nI used to really look forward to fishing, messing around in the woodshop, that kind of thing. My fishing gear’s just been sitting in the garage untouched, and the woodshop feels more like a chore now. Even when my kids want me to play chess, I’ll do it, but I’m kind of just going through the motions.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you find yourself looking forward to?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not much lately. Even when my kids want to play chess, I’ll do it, because I don’t want to let them down, but I’m mostly just going through the motions.\n\nI don’t know if that answers it right, but… yeah, I guess I’ve stopped caring about a lot of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your depressive symptoms seemed to improve for at least a couple of months before getting worse again?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that lasted a couple months.\n\nThere might’ve been days or maybe a week here and there where I seemed a little more okay, or I was keeping up better, but I can’t really point to a stretch where it actually improved for that long and then clearly got worse again. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I wouldn’t say completely unable… I mean, I’m still showing up and trying to handle things. But it’s definitely gotten harder than it should be.

I used to keep schedules and subcontractors straight in my head pretty easily. Now I’ll read the same email three times and still not really take it in, or I’ll be in a meeting and forget what I was about to say. And decisions I used to make in five minutes… I kind of freeze on them now.

It’s embarrassing to admit, because I don’t want to sound incompetent or anything, but yeah, there have been times where taking care of things feels almost beyond me. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?

My kids try to get me to play chess with them, and I do it, but… I’m mostly just going through the motions. I figured maybe that’s worth talking about?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while, honestly. Maybe about a year and a half—closer to a year and seven months, I think. It really started after my wife’s health problems changed everything at home. I kept thinking I’d adjust and it would pass, but… yeah, it’s been going on since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it as sad most of the time, exactly. Maybe kind of flat, or… I don’t know, dulled out? I don’t want to overstate it. Some days I can seem okay, I think. It’s just been hard to feel much of anything about things, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable, or has the pleasure gone away?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wish I could say I still did, but not really. It’s not like I hate those things now or anything. I just… don’t feel much from them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I try not to sit around blaming myself, but it’s hard not to.\n\nI keep thinking I’ve let everybody down—my wife, the kids, the guys at work. Like if I miss an appointment or something goes over budget, or there’s some safety issue on a job, I take it really personally. Probably too personally. It feels like proof that I’m… not doing any of this right. Like maybe I shouldn’t be the one in charge of anything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, a little. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m not trying to scare anybody or anything, but… on those early drives, before anyone else is awake, I’ll catch myself thinking it might be easier if I just didn’t make it there.\n\nAnd sometimes the thought of steering off the road comes up, and that scares me because it feels… too close, I guess. I don’t like saying that out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year and a half?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit, I guess. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches.\n\nFood just doesn’t really do much for me lately. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or wished you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, a little. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m not trying to scare anybody or anything, but… on those early drives, before anyone else is awake, I’ll catch myself thinking it might be easier if I just didn’t make it there.\n\nAnd sometimes the thought of steering off the road comes up, and that scares me because it feels… too close, I guess. I don’t like saying that out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year and a half?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit, I guess. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything, but my belt’s had to come in a couple notches.\n\nFood just doesn’t really do much for me lately. Most days it kind of tastes like cardboard, so I’ll skip breakfast, and then even after being out on a job site all day I’ll just pick at dinner. It’s probably not great, I know.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or restless you feel, or if you've been feeling slowed down compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean… I don’t think I’ve been pacing around or anything like that.\n\nSlowed down… maybe a little? But I don’t know if that’s just normal tiredness or me getting older, you know. I still get myself where I need to be. I probably don’t notice it as much as someone else would. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being flat or dulled out lasted for more than two years now?\n\nPatient: Not more than two years, no. It’s been… about a year and seven months, I think.\n\nIt really started after my wife’s health problems changed things at home. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before like anyone, but this flat, dulled-out feeling… that’s been since then, roughly. Is that the kind of timeframe you’re asking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling really down or depressed before the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any big way. I mean, I’ve had bad weeks here and there, like anybody, but I wouldn’t say I had real periods of being depressed before this.\n\nAt least, not that I’d want to call it that. Does that answer it the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worried or anxious about things?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I’m an anxious person, exactly. I do worry about things, sure, but I’ve always felt like that’s pretty normal.\n\nLately maybe I get a little caught up in my head sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxiety. I can’t really tell if it’s more than anyone else would have. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think there’s been, like, a clear separate period where it got dramatically worse than now.\n\nIt’s more been… kind of steady, I guess. Some days are better or worse, but I don’t know that I’d call it a whole period of being significantly worse. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I try to still do things, especially if the kids ask, but it doesn’t feel the same.\n\nI used to really look forward to fishing, messing around in the woodshop, that kind of thing. My fishing gear’s just been sitting in the garage untouched, and the woodshop feels more like a chore now. Even when my kids want me to play chess, I’ll do it, but I’m kind of just going through the motions.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you find yourself looking forward to?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not much lately. I wish I could say there was something, but I kind of just don’t look forward to the things I used to.\n\nLike, my fishing gear’s still sitting in the garage, and I keep thinking I’ll take it out, but I don’t. The woodshop used to be something I enjoyed, and now it just feels like another chore. Even when my kids want to play chess, I’ll do it, because I don’t want to let them down, but I’m mostly just going through the motions.\n\nI don’t know if that answers it right, but… yeah, I guess I’ve stopped caring about a lot of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your depressive symptoms seemed to improve for at least a couple of months before getting worse again?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that lasted a couple months.\n\nThere might’ve been days or maybe a week here and there where I seemed a little more okay, or I was keeping up better, but I can’t really point to a stretch where it actually improved for that long and then clearly got worse again.
